<issue_description>
[BUG] Issue with the gradients of the expectation value of an operator
### Expected behavior

Hi, I'm trying to compute the gradient of the circuit with respect to the parameters on the operator $\theta_{i,i+1}(\sigma^+_i\sigma^-_{i+1} + \sigma^+_{i+1}\sigma^-_i)$ where $\theta$ is my variable. When I compute the expectation value of the circuit, I can see that individual terms of my operator are differentiable, e.g. for $\theta_0(\sigma^+_0\sigma^-_1 + \sigma^+_1\sigma^-_0)$ I can get a gradient with respect to $\theta_0$ but as soon as I add other terms I get None which I was expecting to get the gradient for every term.

### Actual behavior

Gradient returns ``None``.

### Additional information

I get zero for the gradient when I use autograd or Jax.

### Source code

```shell
import pennylane as qml
import tensorflow as tf
import pennylane.numpy as np

# here s_prod or sum is not necessary +, @ and * works the same
sig_plus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
sig_minus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(-1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
term = lambda w: qml.sum(qml.prod(sig_plus(w) , sig_minus(w + 1)) , qml.prod(sig_plus(w + 1), sig_minus(w)))

@qml.qnode(qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=4), interface="tf")
def circ(phi, theta):
    # This portion of the circuit is random not important
    qml.RY(phi[0], 0)
    qml.RY(phi[1], 1)
    qml.RY(phi[2], 2)
    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
    ###########################
    return qml.expval(theta[0]*term(0) + theta[1]*term(1) ) # only 0 or only 1 works just fine, also tried with qml.sum and qml.s_prod

theta = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))
phi = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))

def cost(phi, theta):
    return circ(phi, theta)

with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
    loss = cost(phi, theta)
tape.gradient(loss, [phi, theta])

#[<tf.Tensor: shape=(3,), dtype=float64, numpy=array([0.00131378, 0.00458278, 0.00750094])>,
# None]
```

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully analyze the issue description:

1. The user is trying to compute gradients of a quantum circuit with respect to parameters on operators
2. They're using operators of the form θ_{i,i+1}(σ⁺_i σ⁻_{i+1} + σ⁺_{i+1} σ⁻_i) where θ is a variable parameter
3. When computing expectation values:
   - Individual terms work fine (e.g., just θ₀ term gives gradient)
   - But when combining multiple terms (θ₀*term(0) + θ₁*term(1)), the gradient returns None
4. The issue occurs specifically with TensorFlow interface
5. With autograd/JAX, they get zero gradients instead of None

Key technical details:
- Using qml.s_prod, qml.sum, qml.prod to construct operators
- Using qml.expval for expectation value
- Interface is set to "tf" 
- Device is "default.qubit.tf"
- Gradient computation uses tf.GradientTape()

The core issue seems to be that when multiple parameterized terms are combined in an expectation value, the gradient computation fails to track gradients properly for some parameters, returning None instead of actual gradient values.

Steps to reproduce:
1. Define sigma plus/minus operators using qml.s_prod and qml.sum
2. Define interaction terms using qml.prod and qml.sum  
3. Create a QNode with default.qubit.tf device and tf interface
4. Return expectation value of sum of parameterized terms
5. Compute gradients using tf.GradientTape()
6. Observe that some gradients are None

The issue is about **gradient computation failure** in PennyLane when computing expectation values of composite operators with multiple parameters. 

**Key Technical Details:**
- **Method names**: `qml.s_prod`, `qml.sum`, `qml.prod`, `qml.expval`, `tf.GradientTape()`
- **Variables**: `theta` parameters, `phi` parameters, `sig_plus`, `sig_minus`, `term` functions
- **Device**: `default.qubit.tf` with `interface="tf"`
- **Error**: Gradients return `None` instead of tensor values for some parameters

**Problem in Clear Terms:**
When constructing composite operators using `qml.sum()` and `qml.prod()` with multiple parameterized terms, the TensorFlow gradient computation fails to track derivatives properly for certain parameters, resulting in `None` gradients instead of actual numerical values.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Define sigma plus/minus operators using `qml.s_prod` and `qml.sum`
2. Define interaction terms combining these operators  
3. Create a QNode with `default.qubit.tf` device and `tf` interface
4. Return expectation value of sum of multiple parameterized terms: `theta[0]*term(0) + theta[1]*term(1)`
5. Compute gradients using `tf.GradientTape()`
6. Observe that gradients for `theta` parameters return `None`

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing
- Ensure proper gradient tracking through operator composition
- Maintain TensorFlow compatibility 
- Test with both single and multiple parameterized terms
- Verify fix doesn't break existing functionality
- Check edge cases with different operator combinations

    14	"""
    15	This file contains the implementation of the SProd class which contains logic for
    16	computing the scalar product of operations.
    17	"""
    18	from typing import Union
    19	from copy import copy
    20	
    21	import pennylane as qml
    22	import pennylane.math as qnp
    23	from pennylane.operation import Operator
    24	from pennylane.ops.op_math.pow import Pow
    25	from pennylane.ops.op_math.sum import Sum
    26	from pennylane.queuing import QueuingManager
    27	
    28	from .symbolicop import ScalarSymbolicOp
    29	
    30	
    31	def s_prod(scalar, operator, lazy=True, do_queue=None, id=None):
    32	    r"""Construct an operator which is the scalar product of the
    33	    given scalar and operator provided.
    34	
    35	    Args:
    36	        scalar (float or complex): the scale factor being multiplied to the operator.
    37	        operator (~.operation.Operator): the operator which will get scaled.
    38	
    39	    Keyword Args:
    40	        lazy=True (bool): If ``lazy=False`` and the operator is already a scalar product operator, the scalar provided will simply be combined with the existing scaling factor.
    41	        do_queue (bool): determines if the scalar product operator will be queued.
    42	            This argument is deprecated, instead of setting it to ``False``
    43	            use :meth:`~.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording`.
    44	        id (str or None): id for the scalar product operator. Default is None.
    45	    Returns:
    46	        ~ops.op_math.SProd: The operator representing the scalar product.
    47	
    48	    .. note::
    49	
    50	        This operator supports a batched base, a batched coefficient and a combination of both:
    51	
    52	        >>> op = qml.s_prod(scalar=4, operator=qml.RX([1, 2, 3], wires=0))
    53	        >>> qml.matrix(op).shape
    54	        (3, 2, 2)
    55	        >>> op = qml.s_prod(scalar=[1, 2, 3], operator=qml.RX(1, wires=0))
    56	        >>> qml.matrix(op).shape
    57	        (3, 2, 2)
    58	        >>> op = qml.s_prod(scalar=[4, 5, 6], operator=qml.RX([1, 2, 3], wires=0))
    59	        >>> qml.matrix(op).shape
    60	        (3, 2, 2)
    61	
    62	        But it doesn't support batching of operators:
    63	
    64	        >>> op = qml.s_prod(scalar=4, operator=[qml.RX(1, wires=0), qml.RX(2, wires=0)])
    65	        AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'batch_size'
    66	
    67	    .. seealso:: :class:`~.ops.op_math.SProd` and :class:`~.ops.op_math.SymbolicOp`
    68	
    69	    **Example**
    70	
    71	    >>> sprod_op = s_prod(2.0, qml.PauliX(0))
    72	    >>> sprod_op
    73	    2.0*(PauliX(wires=[0]))
    74	    >>> sprod_op.matrix()
    75	    array([[ 0., 2.],
    76	           [ 2., 0.]])
    77	    """
    78	    if lazy or not isinstance(operator, SProd):
    79	        return SProd(scalar, operator, do_queue=do_queue, id=id)
    80	
    81	    sprod_op = SProd(scalar=scalar * operator.scalar, base=operator.base, do_queue=do_queue, id=id)
    82	
    83	    if do_queue or do_queue is None:
    84	        QueuingManager.remove(operator)
    85	
    86	    return sprod_op
    87	
    88	
    89	class SProd(ScalarSymbolicOp):
    90	    r"""Arithmetic operator representing the scalar product of an
    91	    operator with the given scalar.
    92	
    93	    Args:
    94	        scalar (float or complex): the scale factor being multiplied to the operator.
    95	        base (~.operation.Operator): the operator which will get scaled.
    96	
    97	    Keyword Args:
    98	        do_queue (bool): determines if the scalar product operator will be queued
    99	            (currently not supported).
   100	            This argument is deprecated, instead of setting it to ``False``
   101	            use :meth:`~.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording`.
   102	        id (str or None): id for the scalar product operator. Default is None.
   103	
   104	    .. note::
   105	        Currently this operator can not be queued in a circuit as an operation, only measured terminally.
   106	
   107	    .. seealso:: :func:`~.ops.op_math.s_prod`
   108	
   109	    **Example**
   110	
   111	    >>> sprod_op = SProd(1.23, qml.PauliX(0))
   112	    >>> sprod_op
   113	    1.23*(PauliX(wires=[0]))
   114	    >>> qml.matrix(sprod_op)
   115	    array([[0.  , 1.23],
   116	           [1.23, 0.  ]])
   117	    >>> sprod_op.terms()
   118	    ([1.23], [PauliX(wires=[0]])
   119	
   120	    .. details::
   121	        :title: Usage Details
   122	
   123	        The SProd operation can also be measured inside a qnode as an observable.
   124	        If the circuit is parameterized, then we can also differentiate through the observable.
   125	
   126	        .. code-block:: python
   127	
   128	            dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=1)
   129	
   130	            @qml.qnode(dev, diff_method="best")
   131	            def circuit(scalar, theta):
   132	                qml.RX(theta, wires=0)
   133	                return qml.expval(qml.s_prod(scalar, qml.Hadamard(wires=0)))
   134	
   135	        >>> scalar, theta = (1.2, 3.4)
   136	        >>> qml.grad(circuit, argnum=[0,1])(scalar, theta)
   137	        (array(-0.68362956), array(0.21683382))
   138	
   139	    """
   140	    _name = "SProd"
   141	
   142	    def __init__(self, scalar: Union[int, float, complex], base: Operator, do_queue=None, id=None):
   143	        super().__init__(base=base, scalar=scalar, do_queue=do_queue, id=id)
   144	
   145	        if (base_pauli_rep := getattr(self.base, "_pauli_rep", None)) and (self.batch_size is None):
   146	            scalar = copy(self.scalar)
   147	            if qnp.get_interface(scalar) == "tensorflow" and not scalar.dtype.is_complex:
   148	                scalar = qnp.cast(scalar, "complex128")
   149	
   150	            pr = {pw: qnp.dot(coeff, scalar) for pw, coeff in base_pauli_rep.items()}
   151	            self._pauli_rep = qml.pauli.PauliSentence(pr)
   152	        else:
   153	            self._pauli_rep = None
   154	
   155	    def __repr__(self):
   156	        """Constructor-call-like representation."""
   157	        return f"{self.scalar}*({self.base})"
   158	
   159	    def label(self, decimals=None, base_label=None, cache=None):
   160	        """The label produced for the SProd op."""
   161	        scalar_val = (
   162	            f"{self.scalar}"
   163	            if decimals is None
   164	            else format(qml.math.toarray(self.scalar), f".{decimals}f")
   165	        )
   166	
   167	        return base_label or f"{scalar_val}*{self.base.label(decimals=decimals, cache=cache)}"
   168	
   169	    @property
   170	    def num_params(self):
   171	        """Number of trainable parameters that the operator depends on.
   172	        Usually 1 + the number of trainable parameters for the base op.
   173	
   174	        Returns:
   175	            int: number of trainable parameters
   176	        """
   177	        return 1 + self.base.num_params
   178	
   179	    def terms(self):  # is this method necessary for this class?
   180	        r"""Representation of the operator as a linear combination of other operators.
   181	
   182	        .. math:: O = \sum_i c_i O_i
   183	
   184	        A ``TermsUndefinedError`` is raised if no representation by terms is defined.
   185	
   186	        Returns:
   187	            tuple[list[tensor_like or float], list[.Operation]]: list of coefficients :math:`c_i`
   188	            and list of operations :math:`O_i`
   189	        """
   190	        return [self.scalar], [self.base]
   191	
   192	    @property
   193	    def is_hermitian(self):
   194	        """If the base operator is hermitian and the scalar is real,
   195	        then the scalar product operator is hermitian."""
   196	        return self.base.is_hermitian and not qml.math.iscomplex(self.scalar)
   197	
   198	    # pylint: disable=arguments-renamed,invalid-overridden-method
   199	    @property
   200	    def has_diagonalizing_gates(self):
   201	        """Bool: Whether the Operator returns defined diagonalizing gates."""
   202	        return self.base.has_diagonalizing_gates
   203	
   204	    def diagonalizing_gates(self):
   205	        r"""Sequence of gates that diagonalize the operator in the computational basis.
   206	
   207	        Given the eigendecomposition :math:`O = U \Sigma U^{\dagger}` where
   208	        :math:`\Sigma` is a diagonal matrix containing the eigenvalues,
   209	        the sequence of diagonalizing gates implements the unitary :math:`U^{\dagger}`.
   210	
   211	        The diagonalizing gates rotate the state into the eigenbasis
   212	        of the operator.
   213	
   214	        A ``DiagGatesUndefinedError`` is raised if no representation by decomposition is defined.
   215	
   216	        .. seealso:: :meth:`~.Operator.compute_diagonalizing_gates`.
   217	
   218	        Returns:
   219	            list[.Operator] or None: a list of operators
   220	        """
   221	        return self.base.diagonalizing_gates()
   222	
   223	    def eigvals(self):
   224	        r"""Return the eigenvalues of the specified operator.
   225	
   226	        This method uses pre-stored eigenvalues for standard observables where
   227	        possible and stores the corresponding eigenvectors from the eigendecomposition.
   228	
   229	        Returns:
   230	            array: array containing the eigenvalues of the operator.
   231	        """
   232	        return self.scalar * self.base.eigvals()
   233	
   234	    def sparse_matrix(self, wire_order=None):
   235	        """Computes, by default, a `scipy.sparse.csr_matrix` representation of this Tensor.
   236	
   237	        This is useful for larger qubit numbers, where the dense matrix becomes very large, while
   238	        consisting mostly of zero entries.
   239	
   240	        Args:
   241	            wire_order (Iterable): Wire labels that indicate the order of wires according to which the matrix
   242	                is constructed. If not provided, ``self.wires`` is used.
   243	
   244	        Returns:
   245	            :class:`scipy.sparse._csr.csr_matrix`: sparse matrix representation
   246	        """
   247	        return self.base.sparse_matrix(wire_order=wire_order).multiply(self.scalar)
   248	
   249	    @property
   250	    def has_matrix(self):
   251	        """Bool: Whether or not the Operator returns a defined matrix."""
   252	        return isinstance(self.base, qml.Hamiltonian) or self.base.has_matrix
   253	
   254	    @staticmethod
   255	    def _matrix(scalar, mat):
   256	        return scalar * mat
   257	
   258	    @property
   259	    def _queue_category(self):  # don't queue scalar prods as they might not be Unitary!
   260	        """Used for sorting objects into their respective lists in `QuantumTape` objects.
   261	        This property is a temporary solution that should not exist long-term and should not be
   262	        used outside of ``QuantumTape._process_queue``.
   263	
   264	        Returns: None
   265	        """
   266	        return None
   267	
   268	    def pow(self, z):
   269	        """Returns the operator raised to a given power."""
   270	        return [SProd(scalar=self.scalar**z, base=Pow(base=self.base, z=z))]
   271	
   272	    def adjoint(self):
   273	        """Create an operation that is the adjoint of this one.
   274	
   275	        Adjointed operations are the conjugated and transposed version of the
   276	        original operation. Adjointed ops are equivalent to the inverted operation for unitary
   277	        gates.
   278	
   279	        Returns:
   280	            The adjointed operation.
   281	        """
   282	        return SProd(scalar=qml.math.conjugate(self.scalar), base=qml.adjoint(self.base))
   283	
   284	    # pylint: disable=too-many-return-statements
   285	    def simplify(self) -> Operator:
   286	        """Reduce the depth of nested operators to the minimum.
   287	
   288	        Returns:
   289	            .Operator: simplified operator
   290	        """
   291	        # try using pauli_rep:
   292	        if pr := self._pauli_rep:
   293	            pr.simplify()
   294	            return pr.operation(wire_order=self.wires)
   295	
   296	        if self.scalar == 1:
   297	            return self.base.simplify()
   298	        if isinstance(self.base, SProd):
   299	            scalar = self.scalar * self.base.scalar
   300	            if scalar == 1:
   301	                return self.base.base.simplify()
   302	            return SProd(scalar=scalar, base=self.base.base.simplify())
   303	
   304	        new_base = self.base.simplify()
   305	        if isinstance(new_base, Sum):
   306	            return Sum(
   307	                *(SProd(scalar=self.scalar, base=summand).simplify() for summand in new_base)
   308	            )
   309	        if isinstance(new_base, SProd):
   310	            return SProd(scalar=self.scalar, base=new_base).simplify()
   311	        return SProd(scalar=self.scalar, base=new_base)
   312

    33	def sum(*summands, do_queue=None, id=None, lazy=True):
    34	    r"""Construct an operator which is the sum of the given operators.
    35	
    36	    Args:
    37	        *summands (tuple[~.operation.Operator]): the operators we want to sum together.
    38	
    39	    Keyword Args:
    40	        do_queue (bool): determines if the sum operator will be queued (currently not supported).
    41	            This argument is deprecated, instead of setting it to ``False``
    42	            use :meth:`~.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording`.
    43	        id (str or None): id for the Sum operator. Default is None.
    44	        lazy=True (bool): If ``lazy=False``, a simplification will be performed such that when any
    45	            of the operators is already a sum operator, its operands (summands) will be used instead.
    46	
    47	    Returns:
    48	        ~ops.op_math.Sum: The operator representing the sum of summands.
    49	
    50	    .. note::
    51	
    52	        This operator supports batched operands:
    53	
    54	        >>> op = qml.sum(qml.RX(np.array([1, 2, 3]), wires=0), qml.PauliX(1))
    55	        >>> op.matrix().shape
    56	        (3, 4, 4)
    57	
    58	        But it doesn't support batching of operators:
    59	
    60	        >>> op = qml.sum(np.array([qml.RX(0.4, 0), qml.RZ(0.3, 0)]), qml.PauliZ(0))
    61	        AttributeError: 'numpy.ndarray' object has no attribute 'wires'
    62	
    63	    .. seealso:: :class:`~.ops.op_math.Sum`
    64	
    65	    **Example**
    66	
    67	    >>> summed_op = qml.sum(qml.PauliX(0), qml.PauliZ(0))
    68	    >>> summed_op
    69	    PauliX(wires=[0]) + PauliZ(wires=[0])
    70	    >>> summed_op.matrix()
    71	    array([[ 1,  1],
    72	           [ 1, -1]])
    73	    """
    74	    if lazy:
    75	        return Sum(*summands, do_queue=do_queue, id=id)
    76	
    77	    summands_simp = Sum(
    78	        *itertools.chain.from_iterable([op if isinstance(op, Sum) else [op] for op in summands]),
    79	        do_queue=do_queue,
    80	        id=id,
    81	    )
    82	
    83	    if do_queue or do_queue is None:
    84	        for op in summands:
    85	            QueuingManager.remove(op)
    86	
    87	    return summands_simp
    88

    98	            This argument is deprecated, instead of setting it to ``False``
    99	            use :meth:`~.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording`.
   100	        id (str or None): id for the sum operator. Default is None.

   101	
   102	    .. note::
   103	        Currently this operator can not be queued in a circuit as an operation, only measured terminally.
   104	
   105	    .. note::
   106	
   107	        This operator supports batched operands:
   108	        >>> op = qml.sum(qml.RX(np.array([1, 2, 3]), wires=0), qml.PauliX(1))
   109	        >>> op.matrix().shape

   117

   119

   121	    >>> summed_op

   123	    >>> qml.matrix(summed_op)
   124	    array([[ 1,  1],
   125	           [ 1, -1]])
   126	    >>> summed_op.terms()
   127	    ([1.0, 1.0], (PauliX(wires=[0]), PauliZ(wires=[0])))
   128	
   129	    .. details::
   130	        :title: Usage Details
   131	
   132	        We can combine parameterized operators, and support sums between operators acting on
   133	        different wires.
   134	
   135	        >>> summed_op = Sum(qml.RZ(1.23, wires=0), qml.Identity(wires=1))
   136	        >>> summed_op.matrix()
   137	        array([[1.81677345-0.57695852j, 0.        +0.j        ,
   138	                0.        +0.j        , 0.        +0.j        ],
   139	               [0.        +0.j        , 1.81677345-0.57695852j,
   140	                0.        +0.j        , 0.        +0.j        ],
   141	               [0.        +0.j        , 0.        +0.j        ,
   142	                1.81677345+0.57695852j, 0.        +0.j        ],
   143	               [0.        +0.j        , 0.        +0.j        ,
   144	                0.        +0.j        , 1.81677345+0.57695852j]])
   145	
   146	        The Sum operation can also be measured inside a qnode as an observable.
   147	        If the circuit is parameterized, then we can also differentiate through the
   148	        sum observable.
   149	
   150	        .. code-block:: python
   151	
   152	            sum_op = Sum(qml.PauliX(0), qml.PauliZ(1))
   153	            dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=2)
   154	
   155	            @qml.qnode(dev, diff_method="best")
   156	            def circuit(weights):
   157	                qml.RX(weights[0], wires=0)
   158	                qml.RY(weights[1], wires=1)
   159	                qml.CNOT(wires=[0, 1])
   160	                qml.RX(weights[2], wires=1)
   161	                return qml.expval(sum_op)
   162	
   163	        >>> weights = qnp.array([0.1, 0.2, 0.3], requires_grad=True)
   164	        >>> qml.grad(circuit)(weights)
   165	        tensor([-0.09347337, -0.18884787, -0.28818254], requires_grad=True)
   166	    """
   167	
   168	    _op_symbol = "+"
   169	    _math_op = math.sum
   170	
   171	    @property
   172	    def hash(self):
   173	        # Since addition is always commutative, we do not need to sort
   174	        return hash(("Sum", frozenset(o.hash for o in self.operands)))
   175	
   176	    @property
   177	    def is_hermitian(self):
   178	        """If all of the terms in the sum are hermitian, then the Sum is hermitian."""
   179	        if self._pauli_rep is not None:
   180	            coeffs_list = list(self._pauli_rep.values())
   181	            if not math.is_abstract(coeffs_list[0]):
   182	                return not any(math.iscomplex(c) for c in coeffs_list)
   183	
   184	        return all(s.is_hermitian for s in self)
   185	
   186	    def terms(self):
   187	        r"""Representation of the operator as a linear combination of other operators.
   188	
   189	        .. math:: O = \sum_i c_i O_i
   190	
   191	        A ``TermsUndefinedError`` is raised if no representation by terms is defined.
   192	
   193	        Returns:
   194	            tuple[list[tensor_like or float], list[.Operation]]: list of coefficients :math:`c_i`
   195	            and list of operations :math:`O_i`
   196	        """
   197	        return [1.0] * len(self), list(self)

   200	        r"""Representation of the operator as a matrix in the computational basis.

    28	def expval(op: Operator):
    29	    r"""Expectation value of the supplied observable.
    30	
    31	    **Example:**
    32	
    33	    .. code-block:: python3
    34	
    35	        dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=2)
    36	
    37	        @qml.qnode(dev)
    38	        def circuit(x):
    39	            qml.RX(x, wires=0)
    40	            qml.Hadamard(wires=1)
    41	            qml.CNOT(wires=[0, 1])
    42	            return qml.expval(qml.PauliY(0))
    43	
    44	    Executing this QNode:
    45	
    46	    >>> circuit(0.5)
    47	    -0.4794255386042029
    48	
    49	    Args:
    50	        op (Observable): a quantum observable object
    51	
    52	    Returns:
    53	        ExpectationMP: measurement process instance
    54	    """
    55	    if not op.is_hermitian:
    56	        warnings.warn(f"{op.name} might not be hermitian.")
    57	
    58	    return ExpectationMP(obs=op)
    59	
    60	
    61	class ExpectationMP(SampleMeasurement, StateMeasurement):
    62	    """Measurement process that computes the expectation value of the supplied observable.
    63	
    64	    Please refer to :func:`expval` for detailed documentation.
    65	
    66	    Args:
    67	        obs (.Operator): The observable that is to be measured as part of the
    68	            measurement process. Not all measurement processes require observables (for
    69	            example ``Probability``); this argument is optional.
    70	        wires (.Wires): The wires the measurement process applies to.
    71	            This can only be specified if an observable was not provided.
    72	        eigvals (array): A flat array representing the eigenvalues of the measurement.
    73	            This can only be specified if an observable was not provided.
    74	        id (str): custom label given to a measurement instance, can be useful for some applications
    75	            where the instance has to be identified
    76	    """
    77	
    78	    @property
    79	    def return_type(self):
    80	        return Expectation
    81	
    82	    @property
    83	    def numeric_type(self):
    84	        return float
    85	
    86	    def _shape_legacy(self, device, shots):  # pylint: disable=unused-argument
    87	        if not shots.has_partitioned_shots:
    88	            return (1,)
    89	        num_shot_elements = sum(s.copies for s in shots.shot_vector)
    90	        return (num_shot_elements,)
    91	
    92	    def shape(self, device, shots):
    93	        if not qml.active_return():
    94	            return self._shape_legacy(device, shots)
    95	        if not shots.has_partitioned_shots:
    96	            return ()
    97	        num_shot_elements = sum(s.copies for s in shots.shot_vector)
    98	        return tuple(() for _ in range(num_shot_elements))

    41	
    42	class DefaultQubitTF(DefaultQubit):
    43	    """Simulator plugin based on ``"default.qubit"``, written using TensorFlow.
    44	
    45	    **Short name:** ``default.qubit.tf``
    46	
    47	    This device provides a pure-state qubit simulator written using TensorFlow.
    48	    As a result, it supports classical backpropagation as a means to compute the Jacobian. This can
    49	    be faster than the parameter-shift rule for analytic quantum gradients
    50	    when the number of parameters to be optimized is large.
    51	
    52	    To use this device, you will need to install TensorFlow:
    53	
    54	    .. code-block:: console
    55	
    56	        pip install tensorflow>=2.0
    57	
    58	    **Example**
    59	
    60	    The ``default.qubit.tf`` is designed to be used with end-to-end classical backpropagation
    61	    (``diff_method="backprop"``) with the TensorFlow interface. This is the default method
    62	    of differentiation when creating a QNode with this device.
    63	
    64	    Using this method, the created QNode is a 'white-box', and is
    65	    tightly integrated with your TensorFlow computation:
    66	
    67	    >>> dev = qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=1)
    68	    >>> @qml.qnode(dev, interface="tf", diff_method="backprop")
    69	    ... def circuit(x):
    70	    ...     qml.RX(x[1], wires=0)
    71	    ...     qml.Rot(x[0], x[1], x[2], wires=0)
    72	    ...     return qml.expval(qml.PauliZ(0))
    73	    >>> weights = tf.Variable([0.2, 0.5, 0.1])
    74	    >>> with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
    75	    ...     res = circuit(weights)
    76	    >>> print(tape.gradient(res, weights))
    77	    tf.Tensor([-2.2526717e-01 -1.0086454e+00  1.3877788e-17], shape=(3,), dtype=float32)
    78	
    79	    Autograph mode will also work when using classical backpropagation:
    80	
    81	    >>> @tf.function
    82	    ... def cost(weights):
    83	    ...     return tf.reduce_sum(circuit(weights)**3) - 1
    84	    >>> with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
    85	    ...     res = cost(weights)
    86	    >>> print(tape.gradient(res, weights))
    87	    tf.Tensor([-3.5471588e-01 -1.5882589e+00  3.4694470e-17], shape=(3,), dtype=float32)
    88	
    89	    There are a couple of things to keep in mind when using the ``"backprop"``
    90	    differentiation method for QNodes:
    91	
    92	    * You must use the ``"tf"`` interface for classical backpropagation, as TensorFlow is
    93	      used as the device backend.
    94	
    95	    * Only exact expectation values, variances, and probabilities are
    96	      differentiable. Creation of a backpropagation QNode with finite shots
    97	      raises an error. If you do try and take a derivative with finite shots on
    98	      this device, the gradient will be ``None``.
    99	
   100	
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me check the qnode.py file to understand how gradients are processed:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30 && grep -n "gradient\|expval" pennylane/qnode.py | head -20</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>91:        diff_method (str or .gradient_transform): The method of differentiation to use in the created QNode.
92:            Can either be a :class:`~.gradient_transform`, which includes all quantum gradient
93:            transforms in the :mod:`qml.gradients <.gradients>` module, or a string. The following
97:              device directly to compute the gradient if supported, otherwise will use
100:            * ``"device"``: Queries the device directly for the gradient.
101:              Only allowed on devices that provide their own gradient computation.
118:            * ``"hadamard"``: Use the analytic hadamard gradient test
120:              :func:`qml.gradients.hadamard_grad <.gradients.hadamard_grad>`.
134:            - ``gradient``: The QNode will attempt to decompose
135:              the internal circuit such that all circuit operations are supported by the gradient
142:            The ``gradient`` strategy typically results in a reduction in quantum device evaluations
148:        grad_on_execution (bool, str): Whether the gradients should be computed on the execution or not.
149:            Only applies if the device is queried for the gradient; gradient transform
150:            functions available in ``qml.gradients`` are only supported on the backward
152:        mode (str): Whether the gradients should be computed on the forward
154:            if the device is queried for the gradient; gradient transform
155:            functions available in ``qml.gradients`` are only supported on the backward
158:            a significant reduction in quantum evaluations during gradient computations.
164:        max_diff (int): If ``diff_method`` is a gradient transform, this option specifies
171:            method. Please refer to the :mod:`qml.gradients <.gradients>` module for details

    14	"""
    15	This submodule defines a base class for symbolic operations representing operator math.
    16	"""
    17	from abc import abstractmethod
    18	from copy import copy
    19	import warnings
    20	
    21	import numpy as np
    22	
    23	import pennylane as qml
    24	from pennylane.operation import Operator
    25	from pennylane.queuing import QueuingManager
    26	
    27	
    28	class SymbolicOp(Operator):
    29	    """Developer-facing base class for single-operator symbolic operators.
    30	
    31	    Args:
    32	        base (~.operation.Operator): the base operation that is modified symbolicly
    33	        do_queue (bool): indicates whether the operator should be
    34	            recorded when created in a tape context.
    35	            This argument is deprecated, instead of setting it to ``False``
    36	            use :meth:`~.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording`.
    37	        id (str): custom label given to an operator instance,
    38	            can be useful for some applications where the instance has to be identified
    39	
    40	    This *developer-facing* class can serve as a parent to single base symbolic operators, such as
    41	    :class:`~.ops.op_math.Adjoint`.
    42	
    43	    New symbolic operators can inherit from this class to receive some common default behavior, such
    44	    as deferring properties to the base class, copying the base class during a shallow copy, and
    45	    updating the metadata of the base operator during queueing.
    46	
    47	    The child symbolic operator should define the `_name` property during initialization and define
    48	    any relevant representations, such as :meth:`~.operation.Operator.matrix`,
    49	    :meth:`~.operation.Operator.diagonalizing_gates`, :meth:`~.operation.Operator.eigvals`, and
    50	    :meth:`~.operation.Operator.decomposition`.
    51	    """
    52	
    53	    _name = "Symbolic"
    54	
    55	    # pylint: disable=attribute-defined-outside-init
    56	    def __copy__(self):
    57	        # this method needs to be overwritten because the base must be copied too.
    58	        copied_op = object.__new__(type(self))
    59	        # copied_op must maintain inheritance structure of self
    60	        # Relevant for symbolic ops that mix in operation-specific components.
    61	
    62	        for attr, value in vars(self).items():
    63	            if attr not in {"_hyperparameters"}:
    64	                setattr(copied_op, attr, value)
    65	
    66	        copied_op._hyperparameters = copy(self.hyperparameters)
    67	        copied_op.hyperparameters["base"] = copy(self.base)
    68	
    69	        return copied_op
    70	
    71	    # pylint: disable=super-init-not-called
    72	    def __init__(self, base, do_queue=None, id=None):
    73	        self.hyperparameters["base"] = base
    74	        self._id = id
    75	        self.queue_idx = None
    76	        self._pauli_rep = None
    77	
    78	        if do_queue is not None:
    79	            do_queue_deprecation_warning = (
    80	                "The do_queue keyword argument is deprecated. "
    81	                "Instead of setting it to False, use qml.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording()"
    82	            )
    83	            warnings.warn(do_queue_deprecation_warning, UserWarning)
    84	
    85	        if do_queue or do_queue is None:
    86	            self.queue()
    87	
    88	    @property
    89	    def batch_size(self):
    90	        return self.base.batch_size
    91	
    92	    @property
    93	    def base(self) -> Operator:
    94	        """The base operator."""
    95	        return self.hyperparameters["base"]
    96	
    97	    @property
    98	    def data(self):
    99	        """The trainable parameters"""
   100	        return self.base.data
   101	
   102	    @data.setter
   103	    def data(self, new_data):
   104	        self.base.data = new_data
   105	
   106	    @property
   107	    def num_params(self):
   108	        return self.base.num_params
   109	
   110	    @property
   111	    def wires(self):
   112	        return self.base.wires
   113	
   114	    @property
   115	    def num_wires(self):
   116	        """Number of wires the operator acts on."""
   117	        return len(self.wires)
   118	
   119	    # pylint: disable=arguments-renamed, invalid-overridden-method
   120	    @property
   121	    def has_matrix(self):
   122	        return self.base.has_matrix
   123	
   124	    @property
   125	    def is_hermitian(self):
   126	        return self.base.is_hermitian
   127	
   128	    @property
   129	    def _queue_category(self):
   130	        return self.base._queue_category  # pylint: disable=protected-access
   131	
   132	    def queue(self, context=QueuingManager):
   133	        context.remove(self.base)
   134	        context.append(self)
   135	        return self
   136	
   137	    @property
   138	    def arithmetic_depth(self) -> int:
   139	        return 1 + self.base.arithmetic_depth
   140	
   141	    @property
   142	    def hash(self):
   143	        return hash(
   144	            (
   145	                str(self.name),
   146	                self.base.hash,
   147	            )
   148	        )
   149	
   150	    def map_wires(self, wire_map: dict):
   151	        # pylint:disable=protected-access
   152	        new_op = copy(self)
   153	        new_op.hyperparameters["base"] = self.base.map_wires(wire_map=wire_map)
   154	        if (p_rep := new_op._pauli_rep) is not None:
   155	            new_op._pauli_rep = p_rep.map_wires(wire_map)
   156	        return new_op
   157	
   158	
   159	class ScalarSymbolicOp(SymbolicOp):
   160	    """Developer-facing base class for single-operator symbolic operators that contain a
   161	    scalar coefficient.
   162	
   163	    Args:
   164	        base (~.operation.Operator): the base operation that is modified symbolicly
   165	        scalar (float): the scalar coefficient
   166	        do_queue (bool): indicates whether the operator should be recorded when created in a tape
   167	            context. This argument is deprecated, instead of setting it to ``False``
   168	            use :meth:`~.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording`.
   169	        id (str): custom label given to an operator instance, can be useful for some applications
   170	            where the instance has to be identified
   171	
   172	    This *developer-facing* class can serve as a parent to single base symbolic operators, such as
   173	    :class:`~.ops.op_math.SProd` and :class:`~.ops.op_math.Pow`.
   174	    """
   175	
   176	    _name = "ScalarSymbolicOp"
   177	
   178	    def __init__(self, base, scalar: float, do_queue=None, id=None):
   179	        self.scalar = np.array(scalar) if isinstance(scalar, list) else scalar
   180	        super().__init__(base, do_queue=do_queue, id=id)
   181	        self._batch_size = self._check_and_compute_batch_size(scalar)
   182	
   183	    @property
   184	    def batch_size(self):
   185	        return self._batch_size
   186	
   187	    @property
   188	    def data(self):
   189	        return (self.scalar, *self.base.data)
   190	
   191	    @data.setter
   192	    def data(self, new_data):
   193	        self.scalar = new_data[0]
   194	        self.base.data = new_data[1:]
   195	
   196	    def _check_and_compute_batch_size(self, scalar):
   197	        batched_scalar = qml.math.ndim(scalar) > 0
   198	        scalar_size = qml.math.size(scalar)
   199	        if not batched_scalar:
   200	            # coeff is not batched
   201	            return self.base.batch_size
   202	        # coeff is batched
   203	        if self.base.batch_size is not None and self.base.batch_size != scalar_size:
   204	            raise ValueError(
   205	                "Broadcasting was attempted but the broadcasted dimensions "
   206	                f"do not match: {scalar_size}, {self.base.batch_size}."
   207	            )
   208	        return scalar_size
   209	
   210	    @property
   211	    def hash(self):
   212	        return hash(
   213	            (
   214	                str(self.name),
   215	                str(self.scalar),
   216	                self.base.hash,
   217	            )
   218	        )
   219	
   220	    @staticmethod
   221	    @abstractmethod
   222	    def _matrix(scalar, mat):
   223	        """Scalar-matrix operation that doesn't take into account batching.
   224	
   225	        ``ScalarSymbolicOp.matrix`` will call this method to compute the matrix for a single scalar
   226	        and base matrix.
   227	
   228	        Args:
   229	            scalar (Union[int, float]): non-broadcasted scalar
   230	            mat (ndarray): non-broadcasted matrix
   231	        """
   232	
   233	    def matrix(self, wire_order=None):
   234	        r"""Representation of the operator as a matrix in the computational basis.
   235	
   236	        If ``wire_order`` is provided, the numerical representation considers the position of the
   237	        operator's wires in the global wire order. Otherwise, the wire order defaults to the
   238	        operator's wires.
   239	
   240	        If the matrix depends on trainable parameters, the result
   241	        will be cast in the same autodifferentiation framework as the parameters.
   242	
   243	        A ``MatrixUndefinedError`` is raised if the base matrix representation has not been defined.
   244	
   245	        .. seealso:: :meth:`~.Operator.compute_matrix`
   246	
   247	        Args:
   248	            wire_order (Iterable): global wire order, must contain all wire labels from the
   249	            operator's wires
   250	
   251	        Returns:
   252	            tensor_like: matrix representation
   253	        """
   254	        # compute base matrix
   255	        if isinstance(self.base, qml.Hamiltonian):
   256	            base_matrix = qml.matrix(self.base)
   257	        else:
   258	            base_matrix = self.base.matrix()
   259	
   260	        scalar_interface = qml.math.get_interface(self.scalar)
   261	        scalar = self.scalar
   262	        if scalar_interface == "torch":
   263	            # otherwise get `RuntimeError: Can't call numpy() on Tensor that requires grad.`
   264	            base_matrix = qml.math.convert_like(base_matrix, self.scalar)
   265	        elif scalar_interface == "tensorflow":
   266	            # just cast everything to complex128. Otherwise we may have casting problems
   267	            # where things get truncated like in SProd(tf.Variable(0.1), qml.PauliX(0))
   268	            scalar = qml.math.cast(scalar, "complex128")
   269	            base_matrix = qml.math.cast(base_matrix, "complex128")
   270	
   271	        # compute scalar operation on base matrix taking batching into account
   272	        scalar_size = qml.math.size(scalar)
   273	        if scalar_size != 1:
   274	            if scalar_size == self.base.batch_size:
   275	                # both base and scalar are broadcasted
   276	                mat = qml.math.stack([self._matrix(s, m) for s, m in zip(scalar, base_matrix)])
   277	            else:
   278	                # only scalar is broadcasted
   279	                mat = qml.math.stack([self._matrix(s, base_matrix) for s in scalar])
   280	        elif self.base.batch_size is not None:
   281	            # only base is broadcasted
   282	            mat = qml.math.stack([self._matrix(scalar, ar2) for ar2 in base_matrix])
   283	        else:
   284	            # none are broadcasted
   285	            mat = self._matrix(scalar, base_matrix)
   286	
   287	        return qml.math.expand_matrix(mat, wires=self.wires, wire_order=wire_order)
   288

    14	"""

    33	class CompositeOp(Operator):
    34	    """A base class for operators that are composed of other operators.
    35	
    36	    Args:
    37	        operands: (tuple[~.operation.Operator]): a tuple of operators which will be combined.
    38	
    39	    Keyword Args:
    40	        do_queue (bool): determines if the operator will be queued.
    41	            This argument is deprecated, instead of setting it to ``False``
    42	            use :meth:`~.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording`.
    43	        id (str or None): id for the operator. Default is None.
    44	
    45	    The child composite operator should define the `_op_symbol` property
    46	    during initialization and define any relevant representations, such as
    47	    :meth:`~.operation.Operator.matrix` and :meth:`~.operation.Operator.decomposition`.
    48	    """
    49	
    50	    _eigs = {}  # cache eigen vectors and values like in qml.Hermitian
    51	
    52	    def __init__(
    53	        self, *operands: Operator, do_queue=None, id=None
    54	    ):  # pylint: disable=super-init-not-called
    55	        self._id = id
    56	        self.queue_idx = None
    57	        self._name = self.__class__.__name__
    58	
    59	        if len(operands) < 2:
    60	            raise ValueError(f"Require at least two operators to combine; got {len(operands)}")
    61	
    62	        self.operands = operands
    63	        self._wires = qml.wires.Wires.all_wires([op.wires for op in operands])
    64	        self._hash = None
    65	        self._has_overlapping_wires = None
    66	        self._overlapping_ops = None
    67	        self._pauli_rep = self._build_pauli_rep()
    68	
    69	        if do_queue is not None:
    70	            do_queue_deprecation_warning = (
    71	                "The do_queue keyword argument is deprecated. "
    72	                "Instead of setting it to False, use qml.queuing.QueuingManager.stop_recording()"
    73	            )
    74	            warnings.warn(do_queue_deprecation_warning, UserWarning)
    75	
    76	        if do_queue or do_queue is None:
    77	            self.queue()
    78	
    79	        self._check_batching(None)  # unused param
    80	
    81	    def _check_batching(self, _):
    82	        batch_sizes = {op.batch_size for op in self if op.batch_size is not None}
    83	        if len(batch_sizes) > 1:
    84	            raise ValueError(
    85	                "Broadcasting was attempted but the broadcasted dimensions "
    86	                f"do not match: {batch_sizes}."
    87	            )
    88	        self._batch_size = batch_sizes.pop() if batch_sizes else None
    89	
    90	    def __repr__(self):
    91	        return f" {self._op_symbol} ".join(
    92	            [f"({op})" if op.arithmetic_depth > 0 else f"{op}" for op in self]
    93	        )
    94	
    95	    def __copy__(self):
    96	        cls = self.__class__
    97	        copied_op = cls.__new__(cls)
    98	        copied_op.operands = tuple(s.__copy__() for s in self)
    99	
   100	        for attr, value in vars(self).items():

   118	    @property

   128	    @data.setter

   130	        """Set the data property"""
   131	        for op in self:
   132	            op_num_params = op.num_params
   133	            if op_num_params > 0:
   134	                op.data = new_data[:op_num_params]
   135	                new_data = new_data[op_num_params:]
   136	
   137	    @property

   140	        return len(self.wires)
   141	
   142	    @property
   143	    def num_params(self):
   144	        return sum(op.num_params for op in self)
   145	
   146	    @property
   147	    def has_overlapping_wires(self) -> bool:
   148	        """Boolean expression that indicates if the factors have overlapping wires."""
   149	        if self._has_overlapping_wires is None:
   150	            wires = []
   151	            for op in self:
   152	                wires.extend(list(op.wires))
   153	            self._has_overlapping_wires = len(wires) != len(set(wires))
   154	        return self._has_overlapping_wires
   155	
   156	    @property
   157	    @abc.abstractmethod
   158	    def is_hermitian(self):
   159	        """This property determines if the composite operator is hermitian."""
   160	
   161	    # pylint: disable=arguments-renamed, invalid-overridden-method
   162	    @property
   163	    def has_matrix(self):
   164	        return all(op.has_matrix or isinstance(op, qml.Hamiltonian) for op in self)
   165	
   166	    def eigvals(self):
   167	        """Return the eigenvalues of the specified operator.
   168	
   169	        This method uses pre-stored eigenvalues for standard observables where
   170	        possible and stores the corresponding eigenvectors from the eigendecomposition.
   171	
   172	        Returns:
   173	            array: array containing the eigenvalues of the operator
   174	        """
   175	        eigvals = []
   176	        for ops in self.overlapping_ops:
   177	            if len(ops) == 1:
   178	                eigvals.append(
   179	                    qml.utils.expand_vector(ops[0].eigvals(), list(ops[0].wires), list(self.wires))
   180	                )
   181	            else:
   182	                tmp_composite = self.__class__(*ops)
   183	                eigvals.append(
   184	                    qml.utils.expand_vector(
   185	                        tmp_composite.eigendecomposition["eigval"],
   186	                        list(tmp_composite.wires),
   187	                        list(self.wires),
   188	                    )
   189	                )
   190	
   191	        return self._math_op(eigvals, axis=0)
   192	
   193	    @abc.abstractmethod
   194	    def matrix(self, wire_order=None):
   195	        """Representation of the operator as a matrix in the computational basis."""
   196

   198	    def overlapping_ops(self) -> List[List[Operator]]:
   199	        """Groups all operands of the composite operator that act on overlapping wires.
   200

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating a Reproduction Script<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import pennylane as qml
import tensorflow as tf
import pennylane.numpy as np

print("Testing gradient issue with Sum and SProd operators...")

# here s_prod or sum is not necessary +, @ and * works the same
sig_plus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
sig_minus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(-1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
term = lambda w: qml.sum(qml.prod(sig_plus(w) , sig_minus(w + 1)) , qml.prod(sig_plus(w + 1), sig_minus(w)))

@qml.qnode(qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=4), interface="tf")
def circ(phi, theta):
    # This portion of the circuit is random not important
    qml.RY(phi[0], 0)
    qml.RY(phi[1], 1)
    qml.RY(phi[2], 2)
    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
    ###########################
    return qml.expval(theta[0]*term(0) + theta[1]*term(1) ) # only 0 or only 1 works just fine, also tried with qml.sum and qml.s_prod

theta = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))
phi = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))

def cost(phi, theta):
    return circ(phi, theta)

print("\n=== Testing Single Term ===")
single_term = theta[0]*term(0)
print("Single term data:", single_term.data)
print("Single term num_params:", single_term.num_params)

print("\n=== Testing Two Terms ===")
two_terms = theta[0]*term(0) + theta[1]*term(1)
print("Two terms data:", two_terms.data)
print("Two terms num_params:", two_terms.num_params)

print("\n=== Computing Gradients ===")
with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
    loss = cost(phi, theta)

gradients = tape.gradient(loss, [phi, theta])
print("Phi gradients:", gradients[0])
print("Theta gradients:", gradients[1])

print("\n=== Debug Information ===")
print("Loss value:", loss)

# Let's also test with individual terms to confirm they work
@qml.qnode(qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=4), interface="tf")
def circ_single_0(phi, theta):
    qml.RY(phi[0], 0)
    qml.RY(phi[1], 1)
    qml.RY(phi[2], 2)
    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
    return qml.expval(theta[0]*term(0))

@qml.qnode(qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=4), interface="tf")
def circ_single_1(phi, theta):
    qml.RY(phi[0], 0)
    qml.RY(phi[1], 1)
    qml.RY(phi[2], 2)
    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
    return qml.expval(theta[1]*term(1))

print("\n=== Testing Individual Terms ===")
with tf.GradientTape() as tape1:
    loss1 = circ_single_0(phi, theta)
gradients1 = tape1.gradient(loss1, [phi, theta])
print("Single term 0 - Phi gradients:", gradients1[0])
print("Single term 0 - Theta gradients:", gradients1[1])

with tf.GradientTape() as tape2:
    loss2 = circ_single_1(phi, theta)
gradients2 = tape2.gradient(loss2, [phi, theta])
print("Single term 1 - Phi gradients:", gradients2[0])
print("Single term 1 - Theta gradients:", gradients2[1])</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/reproduce_issue.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30 && python reproduce_issue.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>2025-09-04 18:40:08.217510: I external/local_xla/xla/tsl/cuda/cudart_stub.cc:31] Could not find cuda drivers on your machine, GPU will not be used.

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Excellent! I've reproduced the issue. Looking at the output:

1. **Single term case**: Both phi and theta gradients are computed correctly
2. **Two terms case**: Phi gradients work, but **theta gradients are None**

From the debug information, I can see that:
- Single term has 9 parameters in its `.data`
- Two terms have 18 parameters in its `.data` 
- All the parameters are properly exposed in the data

But somehow when we have two terms combined, the gradient computation fails for the theta parameters. Let me investigate deeper:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/debug_tape.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import pennylane as qml
import tensorflow as tf
import pennylane.numpy as np

print("Debugging tape parameter detection...")

# here s_prod or sum is not necessary +, @ and * works the same
sig_plus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
sig_minus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(-1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
term = lambda w: qml.sum(qml.prod(sig_plus(w) , sig_minus(w + 1)) , qml.prod(sig_plus(w + 1), sig_minus(w)))

theta = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))
phi = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))

# Let's manually inspect what happens in the tape
dev = qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=4)

@qml.qnode(dev, interface="tf")
def circ_debug(phi, theta):
    qml.RY(phi[0], 0)
    qml.RY(phi[1], 1)
    qml.RY(phi[2], 2)
    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
    observable = theta[0]*term(0) + theta[1]*term(1)
    print("Observable:", observable)
    print("Observable data:", observable.data)
    print("Observable num_params:", observable.num_params)
    return qml.expval(observable)

# Execute the circuit to see the tape structure
result = circ_debug(phi, theta)
print("Result:", result)

# Access the tape directly
tape = circ_debug.tape
print("\n=== TAPE DEBUG INFO ===")
print("Operations:")
for i, op in enumerate(tape.operations):
    print(f"  {i}: {op} - data: {op.data}")

print("\nMeasurements:")
for i, m in enumerate(tape.measurements):
    print(f"  {i}: {m}")
    if m.obs is not None:
        print(f"    Observable: {m.obs}")
        print(f"    Observable data: {m.obs.data}")

print("\nParameter Info:")
for i, par_info in enumerate(tape._par_info):
    print(f"  {i}: {par_info}")

print("\nTrainable Parameters:")
print("  Trainable param indices:", tape.trainable_params)
print("  Number of trainable params:", len(tape.trainable_params))</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/debug_tape.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30 && python debug_tape.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>/workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/pennylane/__init__.py:21: UserWarning: pkg_resources is deprecated as an API.
```python
def _update_trainable_params(self):
    """Set the trainable parameters
    Sets:
        _trainable_params (list[int]): Script parameter indices of trainable parameters
    Call `_update_par_info` before `_update_trainable_params`
    """
    self._trainable_params = list(range(len(self._par_info)))
```

import pennylane as qml
import tensorflow as tf

# here s_prod or sum is not necessary +, @ and * works the same
sig_plus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
sig_minus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(-1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
term = lambda w: qml.sum(qml.prod(sig_plus(w) , sig_minus(w + 1)) , qml.prod(sig_plus(w + 1), sig_minus(w)))

theta = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))
phi = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))

# Let's manually inspect what happens in the tape
dev = qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=4)

@qml.qnode(dev, interface="tf")
def circ_debug(phi, theta):
    qml.RY(phi[0], 0)
    qml.RY(phi[1], 1)
    qml.RY(phi[2], 2)
    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
    observable = theta[0]*term(0) + theta[1]*term(1)
    return qml.expval(observable)

# Execute the circuit to see the tape structure
result = circ_debug(phi, theta)

# Access the tape directly and force trainable params update

Let me add some debug prints to understand what's going wrong:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/debug_tape3.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import pennylane as qml
import tensorflow as tf
import pennylane.numpy as np

print("Debugging the _update process...")

# Monkey patch to add debug prints
original_update_trainable_params = qml.tape.QuantumScript._update_trainable_params

def debug_update_trainable_params(self):
    print(f"DEBUG: _update_trainable_params called!")
    print(f"DEBUG: len(_par_info) = {len(self._par_info)}")
    print(f"DEBUG: _par_info = {self._par_info}")
    result = original_update_trainable_params(self)
    print(f"DEBUG: _trainable_params after update = {self._trainable_params}")
    return result

qml.tape.QuantumScript._update_trainable_params = debug_update_trainable_params

# here s_prod or sum is not necessary +, @ and * works the same
sig_plus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
sig_minus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(-1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
term = lambda w: qml.sum(qml.prod(sig_plus(w) , sig_minus(w + 1)) , qml.prod(sig_plus(w + 1), sig_minus(w)))

theta = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))
phi = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))

# Let's manually inspect what happens in the tape
dev = qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=4)

@qml.qnode(dev, interface="tf")
def circ_debug(phi, theta):
    qml.RY(phi[0], 0)
    qml.RY(phi[1], 1)
    qml.RY(phi[2], 2)
    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
    observable = theta[0]*term(0) + theta[1]*term(1)
    print("Creating expval measurement...")
    measurement = qml.expval(observable)
    print("Measurement created:", measurement)
    return measurement

print("About to execute circuit...")
result = circ_debug(phi, theta)
print("Circuit executed, result:", result)

tape = circ_debug.tape
print("Final trainable params:", tape.trainable_params)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/debug_tape3.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30 && python debug_tape3.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>/workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/pennylane/__init__.py:21: UserWarning: pkg_resources is deprecated as an API. ## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Understanding the Root Cause<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Based on my investigation, I can now clearly identify the problem:

**Root Cause:** The tape's `trainable_params` list is being reset to empty after the initial setup, which prevents TensorFlow from tracking gradients for the observable parameters.

**Evidence:**
1. The `_update_trainable_params()` method is correctly called and sets trainable_params properly (21 parameters total)
2. But by the time we access `tape.trainable_params`, it's empty
3. This explains why gradients return `None` - TensorFlow can't compute gradients for parameters that aren't marked as trainable

**Where the reset happens:** 
The issue is likely in some post-processing step that occurs after tape construction but before gradient computation. This could be:
- Some optimization or processing step that resets trainable parameters
- A filtering mechanism that removes certain types of parameters
- An issue with how the tape handles composite observables vs simple observables

**Why it works for single terms but not combined terms:**
- Single terms might be handled differently in the tape processing
- Or there might be a specific condition that affects composite observables

**The fix approach:**
I need to ensure that the trainable_params are preserved and not reset. Looking at the `_update_trainable_params` method:

```python
def _update_trainable_params(self):
    """Set the trainable parameters
    Sets:
        _trainable_params (list[int]): Script parameter indices of trainable parameters
    Call `_update_par_info` before `_update_trainable_params`
    """
    self._trainable_params = list(range(len(self._par_info)))
```

This should work correctly. The issue might be that this method is being overridden somewhere or there's some condition that prevents it from working with composite observables.

        if any(i > num_params for i in param_indices):
            raise ValueError(f"Quantum Script only has {num_params} parameters.")

        self._trainable_params = sorted(set(param_indices))

Let me create a test to see what `get_trainable_indices` returns for our parameters:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/debug_trainable_indices.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import pennylane as qml
import tensorflow as tf
import pennylane.numpy as np

print("Debugging get_trainable_indices...")

# here s_prod or sum is not necessary +, @ and * works the same
sig_plus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
sig_minus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(-1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
term = lambda w: qml.sum(qml.prod(sig_plus(w) , sig_minus(w + 1)) , qml.prod(sig_plus(w + 1), sig_minus(w)))

theta = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))
phi = tf.Variable(np.random.uniform(0,1,3))

# Let's manually inspect what happens in the tape
dev = qml.device("default.qubit.tf", wires=4)

@qml.qnode(dev, interface="tf")
def circ_debug(phi, theta):
    qml.RY(phi[0], 0)
    qml.RY(phi[1], 1)
    qml.RY(phi[2], 2)
    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
    observable = theta[0]*term(0) + theta[1]*term(1)
    return qml.expval(observable)

# Execute the circuit to see the tape structure
result = circ_debug(phi, theta)

tape = circ_debug.tape
params = tape.get_parameters(trainable_only=False)
print("All parameters:", len(params))
for i, p in enumerate(params):
    print(f"  {i}: {type(p)} - {p}")

print("\nChecking requires_grad for each parameter:")
for i, p in enumerate(params):
    try:
        req_grad = qml.math.requires_grad(p)
        print(f"  {i}: requires_grad = {req_grad}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"  {i}: Error checking requires_grad: {e}")

trainable_indices = qml.math.get_trainable_indices(params)
print(f"\nTrainable indices from get_trainable_indices: {trainable_indices}")

print(f"\nTape trainable_params after override: {tape.trainable_params}")</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/debug_trainable_indices.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30 && python debug_trainable_indices.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>/workspace/PennyLaneAI__pennylane__0.30/pennylane/__init__.py:21: UserWarning: pkg_resources is deprecated as an API. [Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! Now I've found the **exact root cause**:

**THE PROBLEM:** The `get_trainable_indices` function is checking `requires_grad` on ALL parameters extracted from the tape, including:
1. The 3 phi parameters (which are tf.Variables and should be trainable)  
2. The 2 theta parameters (which are tf.Variables and should be trainable)
3. **ALL the constant parameters from the composite observable** (0.5, 1j, -1j, etc.)

Since `requires_grad` returns `False` for ALL parameters (including the TensorFlow variables!), `get_trainable_indices` returns an empty set, which causes `tape.trainable_params` to be set to `[]`.

This is wrong! The TensorFlow variables SHOULD have `requires_grad=True`.     29	def _set_copy_and_unwrap_tape(t, a):
    30	    """Copy a given tape with operations and set parameters"""
    31	    tc = t.copy(copy_operations=True)
    32	    tc.set_parameters(a, trainable_only=False)
    33	    return convert_to_numpy_parameters(tc)
    34	
    35	
    36	def set_parameters_on_copy_and_unwrap(tapes, params):
    37	    """Copy a set of tapes with operations and set parameters"""
    38	    return tuple(_set_copy_and_unwrap_tape(t, a) for t, a in zip(tapes, params))

    41	def _compute_vjp_legacy(dy, jacs):
    42	    # compute the vector-Jacobian product dy @ jac
    43	    # for a list of dy's and Jacobian matrices.
    44	    vjps = []
    45	
    46	    for d, jac in zip(dy, jacs):
    47	        vjp = qml.gradients.compute_vjp(d, jac)

    28	    """Returns True if two tensors are element-wise equal along a given axis.
    29	
    30	    This function is equivalent to calling ``np.all(tensor1 == tensor2, **kwargs)``,
    31	    but allows for ``tensor1`` and ``tensor2`` to differ in type.
    32	
    33	    Args:
    34	        tensor1 (tensor_like): tensor to compare
    35	        tensor2 (tensor_like): tensor to compare
    36	        **kwargs: Accepts any keyword argument that is accepted by ``np.all``,
    37	            such as ``axis``, ``out``, and ``keepdims``. See the `NumPy documentation
    38	            <https://numpy.org/doc/stable/reference/generated/numpy.all.html>`__ for
    39	            more details.
    40	
    41	    Returns:
    42	        ndarray, bool: If ``axis=None``, a logical AND reduction is applied to all elements
    43	        and a boolean will be returned, indicating if all elements evaluate to ``True``. Otherwise,
    44	        a boolean NumPy array will be returned.
    45	
    46	    **Example**
    47	
    48	    >>> a = torch.tensor([1, 2])
    49	    >>> b = np.array([1, 2])
    50	    >>> allequal(a, b)

   398	def requires_grad(tensor, interface=None):
   399	    """Returns True if the tensor is considered trainable.
   400	
   401	    .. warning::
   402	
   403	        The implementation depends on the contained tensor type, and
   404	        may be context dependent.
   405	
   406	        For example, Torch tensors and PennyLane tensors track trainability
   407	        as a property of the tensor itself. TensorFlow, on the other hand,
   408	        only tracks trainability if being watched by a gradient tape.
   409	
   410	    Args:
   411	        tensor (tensor_like): input tensor
   412	        interface (str): The name of the interface. Will be determined automatically
   413	            if not provided.
   414	
   415	    **Example**
   416	
   417	    Calling this function on a PennyLane NumPy array:
   418	
   419	    >>> x = np.array([1., 5.], requires_grad=True)
   420	    >>> requires_grad(x)
   421	    True
   422	    >>> x.requires_grad = False
   423	    >>> requires_grad(x)
   424	    False
   425	
   426	    PyTorch has similar behaviour.
   427	
   428	    With TensorFlow, the output is dependent on whether the tensor
   429	    is currently being watched by a gradient tape:
   430	
   431	    >>> x = tf.Variable([0.6, 0.1])
   432	    >>> requires_grad(x)
   433	    False
   434	    >>> with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
   435	    ...     print(requires_grad(x))
   436	    True
   437	
   438	    While TensorFlow constants are by default not trainable, they can be
   439	    manually watched by the gradient tape:
   440	
   441	    >>> x = tf.constant([0.6, 0.1])
   442	    >>> with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
   443	    ...     print(requires_grad(x))
   444	    False
   445	    >>> with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
   446	    ...     tape.watch([x])
   447	    ...     print(requires_grad(x))
   448	    True
   449	    """
   450	    interface = interface or get_interface(tensor)

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Found it! The issue is in the TensorFlow `requires_grad` implementation. It uses `should_record_backprop([tf.convert_to_tensor(tensor)])` which checks if TensorFlow is currently recording gradients for this tensor. But this only works **when inside a gradient tape context**!

Looking at the example in the docstring:
```python
>>> x = tf.Variable([0.6, 0.1])
>>> requires_grad(x)
False
>>> with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
...     print(requires_grad(x))
True
```

This is exactly what we're seeing. Outside of a gradient tape context, `requires_grad` returns `False` even for TensorFlow variables.

But this is problematic for PennyLane because it needs to determine trainable parameters **before** entering the gradient computation context. The QNode constructor calls `get_trainable_indices` which calls `requires_grad`, but this happens outside any gradient tape.

## THE SOLUTION

The fix should be in how PennyLane handles TensorFlow trainable parameters. Instead of relying solely on `requires_grad` (which is context-dependent for TensorFlow), it should recognize that TensorFlow **Variables** should generally be considered trainable, regardless of the current context.

    def test_incorrect_grad_on_execution(self):
        """Test that an error is raised if a gradient transform
        is used with grad_on_execution"""
        a = tf.Variable([0.1, 0.2])

        dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=1)

        with tf.GradientTape() as t:
            with qml.queuing.AnnotatedQueue() as q:
                qml.RY(a[0], wires=0)
                qml.RX(a[1], wires=0)
                qml.expval(qml.PauliZ(0))

            tape = qml.tape.QuantumScript.from_queue(q)
        with pytest.raises(
            ValueError, match="Gradient transforms cannot be used with grad_on_execution=True"
        ):
            res = execute(
                [tape], dev, gradient_fn=param_shift, grad_on_execution=True, interface="tf"
            )[0]

    def test_grad_on_execution(self, mocker):
        """Test that grad on execution uses the `device.execute_and_gradients` pathway"""
        dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=1)
        a = tf.Variable([0.1, 0.2])
        spy = mocker.spy(dev, "execute_and_gradients")

        with tf.GradientTape() as t:
            with qml.queuing.AnnotatedQueue() as q:
                qml.RY(a[0], wires=0)
                qml.RX(a[1], wires=0)
                qml.expval(qml.PauliZ(0))

            tape = qml.tape.QuantumScript.from_queue(q)
            res = execute(
                [tape],
                dev,
                gradient_fn="device",
                gradient_kwargs={"method": "adjoint_jacobian", "use_device_state": True},
                interface="tf",
            )[0]

        # adjoint method only performs a single device execution, but gets both result and gradient
        assert dev.num_executions == 1
        spy.assert_called()

    def test_no_grad_execution(self, mocker):
        """Test that no grad on execution uses the `device.batch_execute` and `device.gradients` pathway"""
        dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=1)
        spy_execute = mocker.spy(qml.devices.DefaultQubit, "batch_execute")
        spy_gradients = mocker.spy(qml.devices.DefaultQubit, "gradients")
        a = tf.Variable([0.1, 0.2])

        with tf.GradientTape() as t:
            with qml.queuing.AnnotatedQueue() as q:
                qml.RY(a[0], wires=0)
                qml.RX(a[1], wires=0)
                qml.expval(qml.PauliZ(0))

            tape = qml.tape.QuantumScript.from_queue(q)
--
                [tape],
                dev,
                gradient_fn="device",
                grad_on_execution=False,
                gradient_kwargs={"method": "adjoint_jacobian"},
                interface="tf",
            )[0]

        assert dev.num_executions == 1
        spy_execute.assert_called()
        spy_gradients.assert_not_called()

        j = t.jacobian(res, a)
        spy_gradients.assert_called()


class TestCaching:
    """Test for caching behaviour"""

    def test_cache_maxsize(self, mocker):
        """Test the cachesize property of the cache"""
        dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=1)
        spy = mocker.spy(qml.interfaces, "cache_execute")
        a = tf.Variable([0.1, 0.2])

        with tf.GradientTape() as t:
--
                qml.RY(a[0], wires=0)
                qml.RX(a[1], wires=0)
                qml.probs(wires=0)

            tape = qml.tape.QuantumScript.from_queue(q)
            res = execute([tape], dev, gradient_fn=param_shift, cachesize=2, interface="tf")[0]

        t.jacobian(res, a)
        cache = spy.call_args[0][1]

        assert cache.maxsize == 2
        assert cache.currsize == 2
        assert len(cache) == 2

    def test_custom_cache(self, mocker):
        """Test the use of a custom cache object"""
        dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=1)
        spy = mocker.spy(qml.interfaces, "cache_execute")
        a = tf.Variable([0.1, 0.2])
        custom_cache = {}

        with tf.GradientTape() as t:
            with qml.queuing.AnnotatedQueue() as q:
                qml.RY(a[0], wires=0)
                qml.RX(a[1], wires=0)
                qml.probs(wires=0)

            tape = qml.tape.QuantumScript.from_queue(q)
            res = execute([tape], dev, gradient_fn=param_shift, cache=custom_cache, interface="tf")[
                0
            ]

        t.jacobian(res, a)

        cache = spy.call_args[0][1]
        assert cache is custom_cache

        with qml.tape.Unwrap(tape):
            h = tape.hash

        assert h in cache
        assert np.allclose(cache[h], res)

    def test_caching_param_shift(self, tol):
        """Test that, when using parameter-shift transform,
        caching reduces the number of evaluations to their optimum."""
        dev = qml.device("default.qubit", wires=1)
        a = tf.Variable([0.1, 0.2], dtype=tf.float64)

--
                qml.RY(a[0], wires=0)
                qml.RX(a[1], wires=0)
                qml.probs(wires=0)

            tape = qml.tape.QuantumScript.from_queue(q)
            return execute([tape], dev, gradient_fn=param_shift, cache=cache, interface="tf")[0]

        # Without caching, and non-vectorized, 9 evaluations are required to compute
        # the Jacobian: 1 (forward pass) + 2 (backward pass) * (2 shifts * 2 params)
        with tf.GradientTape(persistent=True) as t:
            res = cost(a, cache=None)
        t.jacobian(res, a, experimental_use_pfor=False)
        assert dev.num_executions == 9

        # With caching, and non-vectorized, 5 evaluations are required to compute
        # the Jacobian: 1 (forward pass) + (2 shifts * 2 params)
        dev._num_executions = 0
        with tf.GradientTape(persistent=True) as t:
            res = cost(a, cache=True)
        t.jacobian(res, a)
        assert dev.num_executions == 5

        # In vectorized mode, 5 evaluations are required to compute
        # the Jacobian regardless of caching: 1 (forward pass) + (2 shifts * 2 params)
        dev._num_executions = 0
        with tf.GradientTape() as t:
--
                qml.CNOT(wires=[0, 1])
                qml.var(qml.PauliZ(0) @ qml.PauliX(1))

            tape = qml.tape.QuantumScript.from_queue(q)
            return execute(
                [tape], dev, gradient_fn=param_shift, cache=cache, interface="tf", max_diff=2
            )[0]

        # No caching: number of executions is not ideal
        with tf.GradientTape() as t2:
            with tf.GradientTape() as t1:
                res = cost(params, cache=False)
            grad = t1.gradient(res, params)
        hess1 = t2.jacobian(grad, params)

        if num_params == 2:
            # compare to theoretical result
            x, y, *_ = params * 1.0
            expected = np.array(
                [
                    [2 * np.cos(2 * x) * np.sin(y) ** 2, np.sin(2 * x) * np.sin(2 * y)],
                    [np.sin(2 * x) * np.sin(2 * y), -2 * np.cos(x) ** 2 * np.cos(2 * y)],
                ]
            )
            assert np.allclose(expected, hess1, atol=tol, rtol=0)

--
        assert dev.num_executions == expected_runs_ideal
        assert expected_runs_ideal < nonideal_runs

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! Look at lines 75-77 and 88-89:

```python
# if executing outside a gradient tape, the number of trainable parameters
# cannot be determined by TensorFlow
assert circuit.qtape.trainable_params == []

# the tape is able to deduce trainable parameters
assert circuit.qtape.trainable_params == [0]
```

This confirms that PennyLane **expects** trainable_params to be empty outside of a gradient tape for TensorFlow! This is the intended behavior.

But this creates a problem for our issue. When we do:

```python
with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
    loss = cost(phi, theta)
gradients = tape.gradient(loss, [phi, theta])
```

The QNode execution happens **inside** the gradient tape, so trainable_params should be properly set. # here s_prod or sum is not necessary +, @ and * works the same
sig_plus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
sig_minus = lambda w: qml.s_prod(0.5, (qml.sum(qml.PauliX(w) , qml.s_prod(-1j, qml.PauliY(w)))))
term = lambda w: qml.sum(qml.prod(sig_plus(w) , sig_minus(w + 1)) , qml.prod(sig_plus(w + 1), sig_minus(w)))

    qml.CNOT([0,1])
    qml.CNOT([1,2])
